    'wet_speed', 'overdriving_headlights', 'low_visibility_speed',
    'flashing_yellow_speed',
})

# Lateral-conflict findings: shift away in y.
COLLISION_RULES = frozenset({
    'collision_avoidance', 'lateral_clearance',
    'longitudinal_clearance', 'predicted_collision',
    'sideswipe_risk', 'cut_in', 'overtake_clearance',
    'platoon_gap', 'merge_negotiation',
})

# Lane-membership findings: project to lane center.
LANE_RULES = frozenset({
    'lane_keeping', 'restricted_lane',
    'bike_lane_encroachment', 'sidewalk_encroachment',
})

# Signal / yield / right-of-way / emergency / maneuver-suppression
# findings: stop and hold.
STOP_RULES = frozenset({
    'traffic_light', 'red_light', 'unsafe_yellow_response',
    'flashing_red_no_stop', 'unprotected_turn',
    'rtor_no_sign', 'rtor_no_stop', 'rtor_unclear',
    'green_with_obstacle', 'box_blocking',
    'uncontrolled_priority', 'roundabout_entry', 'turn_yield',
    'left_turn_across', 'emergency_yield', 'crosswalk_yield',
    'implied_crosswalk_yield', 'right_turn_ped_yield',
    'multiway_priority', 'queue_cut', 'protected_lane_misuse',
    'wrong_way', 'emergency_brake', 'fixed_obstacle_contact',
    'unsafe_evasion', 'construction_lane',
    'construction_worker_clearance', 'school_guard_stop',
    'u_turn_prohibited', 'u_turn_sightline',
    'unsafe_overtake', 'no_passing_zone', 'no_passing_sightline',
    'right_overtake', 'blind_spot_maneuver',
})

# Gap findings: gentle slowdown.
FOLLOW_RULES = frozenset({
    'safe_following', 'following_time', 'following_min_gap',
    'tailgating', 'queue_oscillation', 'wave_amplification',
    'rear_end_risk',
})

# Comfort/quality findings: smoothing only.
COMFORT_RULES = frozenset({
    'excess_accel', 'excess_decel', 'lateral_accel',
    'lateral_jerk', 'discontinuous_path', 'turn_radius',
})

# Group dispatch table: group -> corrector method name.
# Order matters only for readability; groups are disjoint.
RULE_GROUPS = {
    'speed': ('_correct_speed_limit', SPEED_RULES),
    'collision': ('_correct_collision', COLLISION_RULES),
    'lane': ('_correct_lane_keeping', LANE_RULES),
    'stop': ('_hold_position', STOP_RULES),
    'follow': ('_correct_safe_following', FOLLOW_RULES),
    'comfort': ('_smooth_point', COMFORT_RULES),
    'min_speed': ('_accelerate_point', frozenset({'min_hwy_speed'})),
}

# Advisory findings: reported, never corrected (fixpoint ignores them).
ADVISORY_RULES = frozenset({'late_signal'})

# Tunable numeric parameters (RQ-1). Values here are the built-in
# defaults; configs/correction_strategies.yaml may override them.
DEFAULT_PARAMS = {
    # Fallback speed target (m/s) when no map/zone limit is known.
    'default_speed_target_ms': 12.5,
    # Fallback following slowdown factor.
    'follow_scale': 0.7,
    # Minimum-speed boost factor (R1.4).
    'min_speed_boost': 1.1,
    # Lateral conflict shift (m).
    'lateral_shift_m': 2.0,
    # Comfort smoothing blend toward neighbor midpoint.
    'smooth_blend': 0.25,
    # Edge smoothing damp factor.
    'smooth_edge_dampen': 0.9,
    # Fallback lane-center y (m) when no map lane matches.
    'fallback_lane_center_y': 0.0,
    # Per-group application cap per correct() call (P-6): bounds
    # single-strategy churn so the fixpoint tries other strategies
    # instead of spinning on one.
    'group_cap': 40,
}


def strategy_for(rule: str):
    """Return (group, method_name) for a finding name.

    Returns (None, None) for advisory rules and ('unknown', None)
    for names outside the catalog (caller applies conservative hold).
    """
    if rule in ADVISORY_RULES:
        return None, None
    for group, (method, rules) in RULE_GROUPS.items():
        if rule in rules:
            return group, method
    return 'unknown', None


def all_known_rules():
    """Every finding name this table knows how to route."""
    known = set(ADVISORY_RULES)
    for _, rules in RULE_GROUPS.values():
        known |= set(rules)
    return known

==> asp-safety/src/corrector.py <==
"""Trajectory Correction Engine.

This module provides the TrajectoryCorrector class that fixes
invalid trajectories to satisfy ASP traffic rules.
"""

import numpy as np
from pathlib import Path
from typing import List, Dict, Tuple, Optional

# Import TrajectoryChecker
try:
    from checker import TrajectoryChecker
except ImportError:
    from src.checker import TrajectoryChecker

# Central rule -> strategy mapping (IF-1). This module must be the
# ONLY place that maps finding names to strategies.
try:
    from correction_strategies import (
        strategy_for, DEFAULT_PARAMS, all_known_rules,
    )
except ImportError:
    from src.correction_strategies import (
        strategy_for, DEFAULT_PARAMS, all_known_rules,
    )

# Continuous optimizer over ASP-emitted bounds (P3/P4). Pure-NumPy
# projected gradient; kept separate from the heuristic fixpoint above.
try:
    from constraint_optimizer import ConstraintOptimizer
except ImportError:
    from src.constraint_optimizer import ConstraintOptimizer


def _is_checker_like(checker) -> bool:
    """Accept TrajectoryChecker by identity or by interface (P-16).

    checker.py is importable as both `checker` and `src.checker`;
    the two module objects make isinstance() fail on a perfectly
    valid checker (dual-identity). Accept either the exact type or
    any object exposing the check()/get_safe_trajectory() interface.
    """
    if isinstance(checker, TrajectoryChecker):
        return True
    return callable(getattr(checker, 'check', None)) and callable(
        getattr(checker, 'get_safe_trajectory', None))


class TrajectoryCorrector:
    """Corrects invalid trajectories to satisfy traffic rules.
    
    This class takes an invalid trajectory and applies corrections
    to make it satisfy all ASP traffic rules.
    
    Attributes:
        checker: TrajectoryChecker instance
        max_iterations: Maximum optimization iterations
    """
    
    def __init__(self, checker: TrajectoryChecker, max_iterations: int = 100,
                 params: Optional[Dict] = None,
                 params_file: Optional[str] = None,
                 on_failure: str = 'raise'):
        """Initialize the corrector.

        Args:
            checker: Initialized TrajectoryChecker instance
            max_iterations: Maximum number of correction iterations
            params: Numeric strategy parameters (overrides file/defaults)
            params_file: Path to YAML params file. Defaults to
                <repo>/configs/correction_strategies.yaml when it exists.
            on_failure: 'raise' (default) raises RuntimeError when the
                fixpoint exhausts max_iterations; 'brake' instead holds
                the trajectory from the earliest remaining violation
                (maximum-braking fallback) and returns it with an
                'emergency_hold_uncorrectable' entry -- the upstream
                uncorrectable-plan contract (flag + hold + takeover).

        Raises:
            TypeError: If checker is not a TrajectoryChecker
            ValueError: If on_failure is neither 'raise' nor 'brake'
        """
        if not _is_checker_like(checker):
            raise TypeError("checker must be a TrajectoryChecker instance")
        if on_failure not in ('raise', 'brake'):
            raise ValueError("on_failure must be 'raise' or 'brake'")

        self.checker = checker
        self.max_iterations = max_iterations
        self.on_failure = on_failure
        self.params = dict(DEFAULT_PARAMS)
        self.params.update(self._load_params(params_file))
        if params:
            self.params.update(params)
    
    @staticmethod
    def _load_params(params_file: Optional[str]) -> Dict:
        """Load numeric params from a YAML file (RQ-1).

        Resolves <repo>/configs/correction_strategies.yaml by default.
        Returns {} when the file is absent or pyyaml is unavailable so
        built-in DEFAULT_PARAMS always apply.
        """
        candidates = []
        if params_file:
            candidates.append(Path(params_file))
        else:
            candidates.append(
                Path(__file__).parent.parent / "configs"
                / "correction_strategies.yaml"
            )
        for path in candidates:
            try:
                if path.exists():
                    import yaml
                    with open(path, 'r') as f:
                        data = yaml.safe_load(f) or {}
                    return {k: v for k, v in data.items()
                            if k in DEFAULT_PARAMS}
            except Exception:
                continue
        return {}
    
    def correct(
        self,
        trajectory: np.ndarray,
        **kwargs
    ) -> Tuple[np.ndarray, List[Dict]]:
        """Correct an invalid trajectory.
        
        Args:
            trajectory: Invalid trajectory, shape (T, 2)
            **kwargs: Arguments for TrajectoryChecker.check()
            
        Returns:
            Tuple of:
                - corrected_trajectory: Shape (T, 2)
                - applied_corrections: List[Dict] with structure:
                    {
                        'rule': str,
                        'timestep': int,
                        'correction_type': str,
                        'original_value': Any,
                        'corrected_value': Any
                    }
                    
        Raises:
            RuntimeError: If correction fails after max_iterations
                (unless on_failure='brake', which holds instead)
        """
        # Check if already valid
        is_safe, violations = self.checker.check(trajectory, **kwargs)

        if is_safe:
            return trajectory.copy(), []

        # Apply corrections iteratively, grouped by timestep. Warnings
        # (advisory) and non-geometric findings (late_signal) are
        # reported but excluded from the fixpoint so the loop terminates.
        corrected = trajectory.copy()
        applied_corrections = []
        group_counts: Dict[str, int] = {}
        group_cap = int(self.params.get('group_cap', 40))
        # Stagnation detection (PR-1): identical violation sets stop
        # improving the plan; break to the fallback early instead of
        # burning max_iterations × full re-grounds on churn.
        stagnant = 0
        last_sig = None

        for iteration in range(self.max_iterations):
            # Check current state
            is_safe, violations = self.checker.check(corrected, **kwargs)

            if is_safe:
                return corrected, applied_corrections

            hard = [v for v in violations
                    if v.get("type", "violation") != "warning"
                    and v["rule"] != "late_signal"]
            if not hard:
                for v in violations:
                    applied_corrections.append({
                        'rule': v['rule'],
                        'timestep': v['time'],
                        'correction_type': 'advisory_no_change',
                        'original_value': corrected[v['time']].tolist(),
                        'corrected_value': corrected[v['time']].tolist(),
                    })
                return corrected, applied_corrections

            sig = frozenset((v['rule'], v.get('vehicle', ''),
                             v.get('vehicle2', ''), v['time'])
                            for v in hard)
            if sig == last_sig:
                stagnant += 1
                if stagnant >= 3:
                    break
            else:
                stagnant = 0
                last_sig = sig

            by_time = {}
            for v in hard:
                by_time.setdefault(v['time'], []).append(v)
            # Stale-snapshot guard (P-16): fixes are computed from one
            # check, but tail-rewriting strategies (hold/speed/boost)
            # invalidate later timesteps in the same pass. Track the
            # rewritten range and skip already-dirty timesteps; they
            # are revisited next iteration with a fresh check.
            # Per-group caps bound single-strategy churn (P-6).
            dirty_from = len(corrected)
            for t in sorted(by_time):
                if t >= dirty_from:
                    continue
                rule = by_time[t][0]['rule']
                group, _ = strategy_for(rule)
                group_key = group or 'advisory'
                if group_counts.get(group_key, 0) >= group_cap:
                    continue
                before = corrected.copy()
                corrected, correction = self._apply_correction(
                    corrected, by_time[t][0], **kwargs
                )
                applied_corrections.append(correction)
                group_counts[group_key] = group_counts.get(group_key, 0) + 1
                if not np.array_equal(before[t:], corrected[t:]):
                    dirty_from = min(dirty_from, t)

        # Final check
        is_safe, violations = self.checker.check(corrected, **kwargs)
        if not is_safe:
            if self.on_failure == 'brake':
                # Maximum-braking fallback: hold from the earliest
                # remaining violation and flag the plan uncorrectable
                # for upstream takeover instead of raising.
                hard = [v for v in violations
                        if v.get("type", "violation") != "warning"
                        and v["rule"] != "late_signal"] or violations
                t0 = max(min(v['time'] for v in hard), 1)
                corrected, _ = self._hold_position(
                    corrected, t0, **kwargs)
                applied_corrections.append({
                    'rule': hard[0]['rule'],
                    'timestep': t0,
                    'correction_type':
                        'emergency_hold_uncorrectable',
                    'original_value': trajectory[
                        min(t0, len(trajectory) - 1)].tolist(),
                    'corrected_value': corrected[
                        min(t0, len(corrected) - 1)].tolist(),
                })
                return corrected, applied_corrections
            raise RuntimeError(
                f"Failed to correct trajectory after {self.max_iterations} iterations"
            )

        return corrected, applied_corrections
    
    def _apply_correction(
        self,
        trajectory: np.ndarray,
        violation: Dict,
        **kwargs
    ) -> Tuple[np.ndarray, Dict]:
        """Apply correction for a specific violation.
        
        Args:
            trajectory: Current trajectory
            violation: Violation dictionary
            
        Returns:
            Tuple of (corrected_trajectory, correction_info)
        """
        rule = violation['rule']
        t = violation['time']

        corrected = trajectory.copy()
        correction_info = {
            'rule': rule,
            'timestep': t,
            'original_value': trajectory[t].tolist()
        }

        # Dispatch via the central rule -> strategy table (IF-1).
        # Every branch changes the trajectory (except advisory
        # late_signal, handled by the caller) so the fixpoint always
        # makes progress.
        group, method_name = strategy_for(rule)

        if group is None:
            # Advisory finding: report, do not touch the trajectory.
            correction_info['correction_type'] = 'advisory_no_change'

        elif group == 'unknown':
            # Unknown future rule: conservative hold guarantees progress.
            corrected, correction_type = self._hold_position(
                corrected, t, **kwargs
            )
            correction_info['correction_type'] = (
                f"hold_unknown_{correction_type}"
            )

        else:
            strategy = getattr(self, method_name)
            corrected, correction_type = strategy(
                corrected, t, **kwargs
            )
            correction_info['correction_type'] = correction_type

        correction_info['corrected_value'] = corrected[t].tolist()

        return corrected, correction_info
    
    def _resolve_speed_target(self, trajectory: np.ndarray, t: int,
                                **kwargs) -> float:
        """Resolve the applicable speed target in m/s (P-16).

        Conservative minimum over: active zone speed limits covering
        the violation point, map speed limits, then the configured
        default. kwargs carries the same map_data/zones passed to
        check(), so no new plumbing is needed.
        """
        candidates = []
        x, y = float(trajectory[t, 0]), float(trajectory[t, 1])
        for zone in kwargs.get('zones') or []:
            try:
                if not zone.get('active', False):
                    continue
                if (zone['x1'] <= x <= zone['x2']
                        and zone['y1'] <= y <= zone['y2']
                        and 'speed_limit' in zone):
                    candidates.append(float(zone['speed_limit']))
            except (KeyError, TypeError):
                continue
        map_data = kwargs.get('map_data') or {}
        for lim in (map_data.get('speed_limit') or {}).values():
            try:
                candidates.append(float(lim))
            except (TypeError, ValueError):
                continue
        candidates.append(float(
            self.params['default_speed_target_ms']))
        return max(0.1, min(candidates))
    
    def _resolve_lane_center(self, trajectory: np.ndarray, t: int,
                             **kwargs) -> float:
        """Resolve the lane-center y in meters for the violation point.

        Uses the nearest map lane centerline covering the point's x
        (axis-aligned segments); falls back to the configured default
        when no map lane matches (P-16: no more hardcoded y=0).
        """
        x = float(trajectory[t, 0])
        best_y = None
        best_dist = None
        map_data = kwargs.get('map_data') or {}
        for segments in (map_data.get('lanes') or {}).values():
            for seg in segments:
                try:
                    x1, y1, x2, y2 = (float(seg['x1']), float(seg['y1']),
                                      float(seg['x2']), float(seg['y2']))
                except (KeyError, TypeError, ValueError):
                    continue
                if y1 == y2 and min(x1, x2) <= x <= max(x1, x2):
                    d = abs(float(trajectory[t, 1]) - y1)
                    if best_dist is None or d < best_dist:
                        best_dist, best_y = d, y1
        if best_y is None:
            return float(self.params['fallback_lane_center_y'])
        return best_y
    
    def _correct_speed_limit(
        self,
        trajectory: np.ndarray,
        t: int,
        **kwargs
    ) -> Tuple[np.ndarray, str]:
        """Correct speed limit violation.
        
        Strategy: Scale trajectory from the violation point onward so
        velocity stays under the applicable limit (map/zone-aware,
        config default otherwise).
        
        Args:
            trajectory: Current trajectory
            t: Violation timestep
            
        Returns:
            Tuple of (corrected_trajectory, correction_type)
        """
        if t == 0:
            return trajectory, "no_change"
        
        # Compute current velocity magnitude at violation timestep
        dx = trajectory[t, 0] - trajectory[t-1, 0]
        dy = trajectory[t, 1] - trajectory[t-1, 1]
        current_speed_ms = np.sqrt(dx**2 + dy**2)  # m/s (positions are in meters)
        
        target_speed_ms = self._resolve_speed_target(
            trajectory, t, **kwargs)
        
        if current_speed_ms > target_speed_ms:
            scale = target_speed_ms / current_speed_ms
            # Scale entire trajectory from the violation point onward
            base_x = trajectory[t-1, 0]
            base_y = trajectory[t-1, 1]
            for t2 in range(t, len(trajectory)):
                rel_x = trajectory[t2, 0] - base_x
                rel_y = trajectory[t2, 1] - base_y
                trajectory[t2, 0] = base_x + rel_x * scale
                trajectory[t2, 1] = base_y + rel_y * scale
        
        return trajectory, "velocity_scaled_to_limit"
    
    def _correct_collision(
        self,
        trajectory: np.ndarray,
        t: int,
        **kwargs
    ) -> Tuple[np.ndarray, str]:
        """Correct collision avoidance violation.

        Strategy: Shift the whole tail laterally (configurable
        distance). A single-point shift manufactures an acceleration
        spike at t+1 that ping-pongs with comfort rules (P-16); rigid
        tail translation preserves every downstream segment shape, so
        only the t-1 -> t entry segment changes.
        """
        shift = float(self.params['lateral_shift_m'])
        trajectory[t:, 1] += shift
        return trajectory, f"lateral_shift_{shift:g}m"
    
    def _correct_lane_keeping(
        self,
        trajectory: np.ndarray,
        t: int,
        **kwargs
    ) -> Tuple[np.ndarray, str]:
        """Correct lane keeping violation.
        
        Strategy: Project to the nearest map lane center (config
        fallback when no map lane matches).
        
        Args:
            trajectory: Current trajectory
            t: Violation timestep
            
        Returns:
            Tuple of (corrected_trajectory, correction_type)
        """
        trajectory[t, 1] = self._resolve_lane_center(
            trajectory, t, **kwargs)
        return trajectory, "project_to_lane_center"
    
    def _correct_safe_following(
        self,
        trajectory: np.ndarray,
        t: int,
        **kwargs
    ) -> Tuple[np.ndarray, str]:
        """Correct safe following distance violation.
        
        Strategy: Reduce speed by 30% to increase following distance.
        
        Args:
            trajectory: Current trajectory
            t: Violation timestep
            
        Returns:
            Tuple of (corrected_trajectory, correction_type)
        """
        if t == 0:
            return trajectory, "no_change"
        
        dx = trajectory[t, 0] - trajectory[t-1, 0]
        dy = trajectory[t, 1] - trajectory[t-1, 1]
        
        scale = float(self.params['follow_scale'])
        trajectory[t, 0] = trajectory[t-1, 0] + dx * scale
        trajectory[t, 1] = trajectory[t-1, 1] + dy * scale

        return trajectory, "velocity_reduction_30pct"

    def _hold_position(
        self,
        trajectory: np.ndarray,
        t: int,
        **kwargs
    ) -> Tuple[np.ndarray, str]:
        """Stop-and-hold: zero velocity at t and freeze the tail.

        Used for all signal, yield, right-of-way, emergency and
        maneuver-suppression findings. Freezing the tail (instead of
        only pinning t) avoids manufacturing a new acceleration spike
        at t+1 that would ping-pong with comfort rules.

        At t<=0 there is no earlier point to hold from, and collapsing
        the whole trajectory to the start point destroys information
        without fixing a start-state violation (e.g. starting inside an
        obstacle/box on red). Return unchanged so the caller can try
        other strategies or honestly report uncorrectable.
        """
        if t <= 0:
            return trajectory, "hold_from_start_noop"
        stop_x, stop_y = trajectory[t-1, 0], trajectory[t-1, 1]
        for t2 in range(t, len(trajectory)):
            trajectory[t2, 0] = stop_x
            trajectory[t2, 1] = stop_y
        return trajectory, "hold_position"

    def _smooth_point(
        self,
        trajectory: np.ndarray,
        t: int,
        **kwargs
    ) -> Tuple[np.ndarray, str]:
        """Gently pull a point 25% toward its neighbors' midpoint.

        Used for comfort/quality findings (R12.x, R7.5).
        """
        n = len(trajectory)
        dampen = float(self.params['smooth_edge_dampen'])
        blend = float(self.params['smooth_blend'])
        if n < 3 or t <= 0 or t >= n - 1:
            if t > 0:
                dx = trajectory[t, 0] - trajectory[t-1, 0]
                dy = trajectory[t, 1] - trajectory[t-1, 1]
                trajectory[t, 0] = trajectory[t-1, 0] + dx * dampen
                trajectory[t, 1] = trajectory[t-1, 1] + dy * dampen
            return trajectory, "smooth_edge_dampen"
        mid_x = (trajectory[t-1, 0] + trajectory[t+1, 0]) / 2.0
        mid_y = (trajectory[t-1, 1] + trajectory[t+1, 1]) / 2.0
        trajectory[t, 0] += (mid_x - trajectory[t, 0]) * blend
        trajectory[t, 1] += (mid_y - trajectory[t, 1]) * blend
        return trajectory, "smooth_midpoint_blend"

    def _accelerate_point(
        self,
        trajectory: np.ndarray,
        t: int,
        **kwargs
    ) -> Tuple[np.ndarray, str]:
        """Raise speed toward the posted minimum (R1.4).

        Targeted tail stretch: scale the tail from t onward so the
        slowest downstream segment reaches the strictest applicable
        min_speed_limit (mirrors _resolve_speed_target). A blind fixed
        boost overshoots the speed cap on healthy segments and
        ping-pongs against the scale-down strategy (T-8). From a
        near-stop (min segment < 0.5 m/s) the ratio is unbounded, so
        fall back to one gentle fixed boost per visit.
        """
        if t == 0:
            return trajectory, "no_change"
        segs = [float(np.linalg.norm(trajectory[k] - trajectory[k - 1]))
                for k in range(t, len(trajectory))]
        if not segs:
            return trajectory, "no_change"
        cur_min = min(segs)
        if cur_min < 0.5:
            boost = float(self.params['min_speed_boost'])
        else:
            target = self._resolve_min_target(**kwargs)
            if target <= cur_min:
                return trajectory, "min_target_met"
            boost = min(target / cur_min,
                        float(self.params['min_speed_boost']) * 4)
        base_x, base_y = trajectory[t-1, 0], trajectory[t-1, 1]
        for t2 in range(t, len(trajectory)):
            trajectory[t2, 0] = base_x + (trajectory[t2, 0] - base_x) * boost
            trajectory[t2, 1] = base_y + (trajectory[t2, 1] - base_y) * boost
        return trajectory, "velocity_boost_10pct"

    def _resolve_min_target(self, **kwargs) -> float:
        """Resolve the applicable minimum speed in m/s.

        Strictest (max) map min_speed_limit; 0.0 when none is mapped
        (callers then fall back to a gentle fixed boost).
        """
        best = 0.0
        map_data = kwargs.get('map_data') or {}
        for lim in (map_data.get('min_speed_limit') or {}).values():
            try:
                best = max(best, float(lim))
            except (TypeError, ValueError):
                continue
        return best


class CorrectorQP:
    """ASP-constrained corrector (P4): solver bounds + continuous repair.

    Check with constraints -> projected-gradient solve -> symbolic
    re-validation, instead of the heuristic per-rule fixpoint. Falls
    back to TrajectoryCorrector (same on_failure contract) when the
    constrained solve cannot clear the violations.

    Attributes:
        checker: TrajectoryChecker instance (must support
            check(include_constraints=True) from P1).
        optimizer: ConstraintOptimizer instance.
        max_rounds: solve + re-validate rounds before fallback.
        on_failure: 'raise' or 'brake' (mirrors TrajectoryCorrector).
    """

    def __init__(self, checker, optimizer=None,
                 optimizer_params: Optional[Dict] = None,
                 max_rounds: int = 3,
                 on_failure: str = 'raise',
                 heuristic_fallback: bool = True,
                 bounds: str = 'full'):
        """Initialize the QP corrector.

        Args:
            checker: Initialized TrajectoryChecker instance.
            optimizer: ConstraintOptimizer (built from optimizer_params
                when omitted).
            optimizer_params: Per-solve knobs (lr, steps, tol_cm,
                lane_pull); also forwarded per correct() call.
            max_rounds: Maximum solve + re-validate rounds.
            on_failure: 'raise' (RuntimeError) or 'brake' (heuristic
                corrector's flagged emergency hold).
            heuristic_fallback: If True (default), delegate to
                TrajectoryCorrector when the constrained solve fails;
                if False, apply on_failure directly (raise, or hold
                from the earliest remaining violation).
            bounds: Bound-family ablation switch (P6): 'full' (all
                emitted bounds), 'caps' (v_max/v_min/a_max only),
                'boxes' (x_bounds/y_bounds/lane_center only). Pure
                Python filtering of the constraints dict -- no .lp
                changes -- so subset runs stay comparable.

        Raises:
            TypeError: If checker is not checker-like.
            ValueError: If on_failure is neither 'raise' nor 'brake',
                or bounds is not a known family.
        """
        if not _is_checker_like(checker):
            raise TypeError("checker must be a TrajectoryChecker instance")
        if on_failure not in ('raise', 'brake'):
            raise ValueError("on_failure must be 'raise' or 'brake'")
        if bounds not in ('full', 'caps', 'boxes'):
            raise ValueError("bounds must be 'full', 'caps' or 'boxes'")
        self.checker = checker
        self.optimizer = optimizer or ConstraintOptimizer(
            params=optimizer_params)
        self.optimizer_params = optimizer_params or {}
        self.max_rounds = max(1, int(max_rounds))
        self.on_failure = on_failure
        self.heuristic_fallback = heuristic_fallback
        self.bounds = bounds

    @staticmethod
    def _filter_bounds(constraints: Dict[str, list],
                       bounds: str) -> Dict[str, list]:
        """Keep one bound family for ablation (P6).

        'caps' (limit-type: v_max/v_min/a_max) vs 'boxes'
        (region-type: x_bounds/y_bounds/lane_center). 'full' returns
        the dict unchanged.
        """
        if bounds == 'full':
            return constraints
        keep = {'caps': {'v_max', 'v_min', 'a_max'},
                'boxes': {'x_bounds', 'y_bounds', 'lane_center'}}[bounds]
        return {k: (v if k in keep else []) for k, v in constraints.items()}

    def correct(
        self,
        trajectory: np.ndarray,
        **kwargs
    ) -> Tuple[np.ndarray, List[Dict]]:
        """Correct via constrained optimization with re-validation.

        Args:
            trajectory: Invalid trajectory, shape (T, 2).
            **kwargs: Arguments for TrajectoryChecker.check()
                (include_constraints is managed internally and never
                forwarded, so the heuristic fallback always sees the
                legacy 2-tuple contract).

        Returns:
            Tuple of (corrected_trajectory, applied_corrections) in
            the same 5-key schema as TrajectoryCorrector.correct.

        Raises:
            RuntimeError: If the solve fails and on_failure='raise'.
        """
        kwargs.pop('include_constraints', None)
        kwargs.pop('include_internal', None)
        orig = np.asarray(trajectory, dtype=float)

        is_safe, violations, constraints = self.checker.check(
            orig, include_constraints=True, **kwargs)
        if is_safe:
            return orig.copy(), []
        constraints = self._filter_bounds(constraints, self.bounds)

        current = orig.copy()
        rounds = 0
        last_info: Dict = {}
        # P-23 increment 2: accumulate bounds across rounds. Replacing
        # the constraint set each round let a later round undo an
        # earlier fix (the solved plan moved back toward the original
        # and re-violated round 1's rule) -- the measured whack-a-mole.
        # Merging is sound: the optimizer's indexing keeps the tightest
        # box / minimum cap per timestep, so accumulation only tightens.
        accum = dict(constraints)
        for rounds in range(1, self.max_rounds + 1):
            solved, info = self.optimizer.solve(
                current, accum, params=self.optimizer_params)
            last_info = info
            ok, v2, c2 = self.checker.check(
                solved, include_constraints=True, **kwargs)
            if ok:
                return solved, self._qp_entries(orig, solved, violations,
                                                rounds, info)
            current = solved
            accum = self._merge_constraints(
                accum, self._filter_bounds(c2, self.bounds))

        # Constrained solve could not clear the violations.
        if self.heuristic_fallback:
            heur = TrajectoryCorrector(
                self.checker, on_failure=self.on_failure)
            fixed, entries = heur.correct(orig, **kwargs)
            attempt = {
                'rule': 'qp_optimization',
                'timestep': int(min(v['time'] for v in violations)),
                'correction_type': (
                    f"qp_infeasible_{rounds}r_fallback_"
                    f"{last_info.get('status', 'unknown')}"),
                'original_value': orig[
                    min(int(min(v['time'] for v in violations)),
                        len(orig) - 1)].tolist(),
                'corrected_value': fixed[
                    min(int(min(v['time'] for v in violations)),
                        len(fixed) - 1)].tolist(),
            }
            return fixed, [attempt] + entries
        if self.on_failure == 'brake':
            heur = TrajectoryCorrector(
                self.checker, on_failure='brake')
            fixed, entries = heur.correct(orig, **kwargs)
            return fixed, entries
        raise RuntimeError(
            f"CorrectorQP failed after {rounds} constrained rounds "
            f"({last_info.get('status', 'unknown')})"
        )

    @staticmethod
    def _merge_constraints(old: Dict[str, list],
                           new: Dict[str, list]) -> Dict[str, list]:
        """Union two grouped constraint dicts (P-23 increment 2).

        Concatenates per-family entry lists; the optimizer's indexing
        keeps the tightest box / minimum cap per timestep, so the union
        only ever tightens. Entries are plain tuples, so no aliasing:
        the merged dict shares no mutable state with either input.
        """
        merged: Dict[str, list] = {}
        for key in set(old) | set(new):
            merged[key] = list(old.get(key, [])) + list(new.get(key, []))
        return merged

    @staticmethod
    def _qp_entries(orig: np.ndarray, solved: np.ndarray,
                    violations: List[Dict], rounds: int,
                    info: Dict) -> List[Dict]:
        """One applied entry per moved timestep (1 cm threshold)."""
        entries = []
        moved = [t for t in range(1, len(orig))
                 if float(np.linalg.norm(solved[t] - orig[t])) > 0.01]
        if not moved:
            moved = [int(min(v['time'] for v in violations))]
        for t in moved:
            t = max(1, min(int(t), len(orig) - 1))
            entries.append({
                'rule': 'qp_optimization',
                'timestep': t,
                'correction_type': (
                    f"projected_gradient_{info.get('status', 'unknown')}"
                    f"_{rounds}r_{info.get('iterations', 0)}it"),
                'original_value': orig[t].tolist(),
                'corrected_value': solved[t].tolist(),
            })
        return entries

==> asp-safety/src/data_loader.py <==
"""Data loader for nuScenes dataset.

This module provides data loading functionality for the nuScenes dataset.
If nuScenes is not available, it generates placeholder data for testing.
"""

import numpy as np
from typing import Dict, List, Optional, Tuple
from pathlib import Path

# Camera order expected by the planner contract.
NUSC_CAM_ORDER = [
    'CAM_FRONT', 'CAM_FRONT_LEFT', 'CAM_FRONT_RIGHT',
    'CAM_BACK', 'CAM_BACK_LEFT', 'CAM_BACK_RIGHT',
]

# Planner input resolution (W, H).
PLANNER_W, PLANNER_H = 704, 256

# Real-map extraction budgets (perf vs completeness, forward item 3).
# Tile/crosswalk counts dominate real-scene grounding cost; the P-5
# verdict ledger lives in experiments/sweep_verdict.md.
MAP_QUERY_RADIUS = 40.0
MAP_LANE_TOKENS = 32
MAP_TILE_CELLS = 48
MAP_CROSSWALK_POLYS = 8
MAP_WALKWAY_POLYS = 8
MAP_CENTERLINE_RES = 2.0


def _pose_to_mat(translation, rotation) -> np.ndarray:
    """Compose a 4x4 transform from nuScenes translation + quaternion.

    nuScenes quaternions are (w, x, y, z).
    """
    from pyquaternion import Quaternion
    mat = np.eye(4, dtype=np.float64)
    mat[:3, :3] = Quaternion(rotation).rotation_matrix
    mat[:3, 3] = np.asarray(translation, dtype=np.float64)
    return mat


def estimate_ego_status(
    prev_ego2global: np.ndarray,
    ego2global: np.ndarray,
    dt: float,
) -> np.ndarray:
    """Estimate the 18-dim ego dynamic state from consecutive ego poses.

    Layout follows the upstream converter
    (SparseDrive/tools/data_converter/nuscenes_converter.py::get_ego_status):
    [ax,ay,az, wx,wy,wz, vx,vy,vz, steer, 0*8]. Upstream falls back to
    all-zeros when CAN bus is absent (always, on mini); pose-derived
    planar velocity is strictly more informative and uses the same
    indices the checkpoint was trained on (status[6]=vx feeds the ego
    anchor in instance_queue.prepare_planning).

    Only planar velocity is observable from two poses; acceleration,
    angular rate, steering and padding stay zero.
    """
    status = np.zeros(18, dtype=np.float32)
    if dt <= 0:
        return status
    rel = np.linalg.inv(prev_ego2global.astype(np.float64)) @ \
        ego2global.astype(np.float64)
    status[6] = rel[0, 3] / dt  # vx: forward, m/s (ego frame x=forward)
    status[7] = rel[1, 3] / dt  # vy: left, m/s (ego frame y=left)
    return status


class NuScenesLoader:
    """Loader for nuScenes dataset.
    
    This class loads camera images, calibration data, and ground truth
    from the nuScenes dataset. If the dataset is not available,
    it generates placeholder data for testing.
    
    Attributes:
        data_dir: Path to nuScenes data directory
        version: Dataset version
        use_placeholder: Whether using placeholder data
    """
    
    def __init__(self, data_dir: str, version: str = "v1.0-mini"):
        """Initialize the data loader.
        
        Args:
            data_dir: Path to nuScenes data directory
            version: Dataset version ("v1.0-mini", "v1.0-trainval", "v1.0-test")
            
        Raises:
            ValueError: If version not in valid list
        """
        valid_versions = ["v1.0-mini", "v1.0-trainval", "v1.0-test"]
        if version not in valid_versions:
            raise ValueError(f"Invalid version: {version}. Must be one of {valid_versions}")
        
        self.data_dir = Path(data_dir)
        self.version = version
        self.use_placeholder = not self.data_dir.exists()
        
        if self.use_placeholder:
            print(f"nuScenes not found at {data_dir}. Using placeholder data.")
            self.num_scenes = 10  # Placeholder scenes
        else:
            # Real nuScenes scene count when devkit is installed;
            # falls back to 10 with a warning otherwise.
            try:
                from nuscenes.nuscenes import NuScenes
                self.nusc = NuScenes(
                    version=version, dataroot=str(data_dir))
                self.num_scenes = len(self.nusc.scene)
            except Exception as e:
                print(f"nuScenes devkit unavailable ({e}); "
                      f"using placeholder data.")
                self.use_placeholder = True
                self.num_scenes = 10
    
    def get_scene_sample(self, scene_idx: int = 0) -> Dict[str, np.ndarray]:
        """Get a sample from the dataset.
        
        Args:
            scene_idx: Index of scene to load (0-indexed)
            
        Returns:
            Dict with keys:
                'images': np.ndarray, shape (6, 3, 256, 704), uint8
                    Camera images in order:
                    [CAM_FRONT, CAM_FRONT_LEFT, CAM_FRONT_RIGHT,
                     CAM_BACK, CAM_BACK_LEFT, CAM_BACK_RIGHT]
                'intrinsics': np.ndarray, shape (6, 3, 3), float32
                    Camera intrinsic matrices
                'extrinsics': np.ndarray, shape (6, 4, 4), float32
                    Camera extrinsic matrices (camera-to-vehicle)
                'ego2global': np.ndarray, shape (4, 4), float32
                    Vehicle-to-global transformation
                'timestamp': float
                    Unix timestamp
                    
        Raises:
            IndexError: If scene_idx out of range
        """
        if scene_idx < 0 or scene_idx >= self.num_scenes:
            raise IndexError(
                f"scene_idx {scene_idx} out of range [0, {self.num_scenes})"
            )
        
        if self.use_placeholder:
            return self._generate_placeholder_sample(scene_idx)
        else:
            return self._load_nuscenes_sample(scene_idx)
    
    def _generate_placeholder_sample(self, scene_idx: int) -> Dict[str, np.ndarray]:
        """Generate placeholder sample for testing.
        
        Args:
            scene_idx: Scene index (used for seed)
            
        Returns:
            Placeholder sample dictionary
        """
        # Use scene_idx as seed for reproducibility
        np.random.seed(scene_idx)
        
        # Generate random images (6 cameras, 3 channels, 256x704)
        images = np.random.randint(
            0, 255, (6, 3, 256, 704), dtype=np.uint8
        )
        
        # Generate identity intrinsics
        intrinsics = np.eye(3, dtype=np.float32)[np.newaxis].repeat(6, axis=0)
        # Set focal length and principal point
        intrinsics[:, 0, 0] = 400.0  # fx
        intrinsics[:, 1, 1] = 400.0  # fy
        intrinsics[:, 0, 2] = 352.0  # cx
        intrinsics[:, 1, 2] = 128.0  # cy
        
        # Generate identity extrinsics
        extrinsics = np.eye(4, dtype=np.float32)[np.newaxis].repeat(6, axis=0)
        
        # Generate ego2global
        ego2global = np.eye(4, dtype=np.float32)
        ego2global[0, 3] = scene_idx * 10.0  # x offset
        
        # Generate timestamp
        timestamp = 1234567890.0 + scene_idx * 0.5
        
        return {
            'images': images,
            'intrinsics': intrinsics,
            'extrinsics': extrinsics,
            'ego2global': ego2global,
            'timestamp': timestamp
        }
    
    def _load_nuscenes_sample(self, scene_idx: int) -> Dict[str, np.ndarray]:
        """Load an actual nuScenes sample (MC-3).

        Uses the middle sample of the scene for variety. Images are read
        with cv2 (BGR->RGB), resized to the planner resolution, with
        intrinsics scaled to match. Extrinsics are lidar-to-camera
        4x4 transforms (cam_T_lidar), matching _build_projection_mat.
        The chosen sample token is cached so trajectory/map calls for
        the same scene stay consistent.
        """
        scene = self.nusc.scene[scene_idx]
        # Middle sample: deterministic, avoids scene-boundary effects.
        nbr = scene['nbr_samples']
        target = nbr // 2
        token = scene['first_sample_token']
        for _ in range(target):
            token = self.nusc.get('sample', token)['next']
        self._sample_tokens = getattr(self, '_sample_tokens', {})
        self._sample_tokens[scene_idx] = token
        return self._load_sample_by_token(token)

    def get_scene_frames(
        self, scene_idx: int, n_past: int = 4
    ) -> list:
        """Load consecutive frames ending at the scene's canonical sample.

        Walks BACKWARD from the middle sample (via sample['prev']) so
        history always exists, and returns oldest->newest sample dicts
        (same schema as get_scene_sample). Powers the N-2 warm-queue
        harness: feed all but the last frame to warm the temporal banks,
        evaluate on the last.

        Falls back to repeating the canonical sample if the scene start
        is reached (mini scenes are short).
        """
        if scene_idx < 0 or scene_idx >= self.num_scenes:
            raise IndexError(
                f"scene_idx {scene_idx} out of range [0, {self.num_scenes})"
            )
        if self.use_placeholder:
            base = self._generate_placeholder_sample(scene_idx)
            return [dict(base) for _ in range(n_past + 1)]

        # Ensure the canonical token is cached.
        self._load_nuscenes_sample(scene_idx)
        token = self._sample_tokens[scene_idx]
        tokens = [token]
        for _ in range(n_past):
            prev = self.nusc.get('sample', tokens[-1])['prev']
            tokens.append(prev if prev else tokens[-1])
        tokens.reverse()
        return [self._load_sample_by_token(t) for t in tokens]

    def _load_sample_by_token(self, token: str) -> Dict[str, np.ndarray]:
        """Load one sample dict by sample token (shared by canonical + frames)."""
        import cv2

        sample = self.nusc.get('sample', token)
        images, intrinsics, extrinsics = [], [], []
        ego2global = None
        timestamp = None

        # Lidar pose for cam_T_lidar composition.
        lidar_token = sample['data']['LIDAR_TOP']
        lidar_sd = self.nusc.get('sample_data', lidar_token)
        lidar_cal = self.nusc.get(
            'calibrated_sensor', lidar_sd['calibrated_sensor_token'])
        lidar_ego = self.nusc.get('ego_pose', lidar_sd['ego_pose_token'])
        ego_T_lidar = _pose_to_mat(
            lidar_cal['translation'], lidar_cal['rotation'])
        # Canonical ego frame + timestamp come from LIDAR_TOP.
        ego2global = _pose_to_mat(
            lidar_ego['translation'],
            lidar_ego['rotation']).astype(np.float32)
        timestamp = float(lidar_sd['timestamp']) / 1e6

        for cam in NUSC_CAM_ORDER:
            sd = self.nusc.get('sample_data', sample['data'][cam])
            img_path = str(self.data_dir / sd['filename'])
            img = cv2.imread(img_path, cv2.IMREAD_COLOR)
            if img is None:
                raise FileNotFoundError(f"image missing: {img_path}")
            img = cv2.cvtColor(img, cv2.COLOR_BGR2RGB)
            h0, w0 = img.shape[:2]
            img = cv2.resize(img, (PLANNER_W, PLANNER_H),
                             interpolation=cv2.INTER_LINEAR)
            images.append(img.transpose(2, 0, 1))  # HWC -> CHW

            cal = self.nusc.get(
                'calibrated_sensor', sd['calibrated_sensor_token'])
            K = np.array(cal['camera_intrinsic'], dtype=np.float32)
            # Scale intrinsics to the resized resolution.
            K[0, :] *= PLANNER_W / w0
            K[1, :] *= PLANNER_H / h0
            intrinsics.append(K)

            ego_T_cam = _pose_to_mat(
                cal['translation'], cal['rotation'])
            cam_T_lidar = np.linalg.inv(ego_T_cam) @ ego_T_lidar
            extrinsics.append(cam_T_lidar.astype(np.float32))

        return {
            'images': np.stack(images).astype(np.uint8),
            'intrinsics': np.stack(intrinsics).astype(np.float32),
            'extrinsics': np.stack(extrinsics).astype(np.float32),
            'ego2global': ego2global,
            'timestamp': timestamp,
        }
    
    def get_trajectory(self, scene_idx: int = 0) -> np.ndarray:
        """Get ground truth trajectory for a scene.
        
        Args:
            scene_idx: Index of scene
            
        Returns:
            np.ndarray: Shape (T, 2), dtype float32
                T = up to 6 timesteps
                Values = [x, y] positions in vehicle-local coordinates
        """
        if self.use_placeholder:
            return self._generate_placeholder_trajectory(scene_idx)
        else:
            return self._load_nuscenes_trajectory(scene_idx)
    
    def _load_nuscenes_trajectory(self, scene_idx: int) -> np.ndarray:
        """Load ground-truth ego trajectory (MC-3).

        Walks the sample `next` chain from the cached scene sample,
        taking every 2nd pose (nuScenes is 2 Hz; STEP_DT=1.0 s per
        checker.STEP_DT). Poses are expressed in the first pose's
        local frame (x=forward, y=left). Returns up to 6 waypoints;
        fewer if the scene ends first.
        """
        tokens = getattr(self, '_sample_tokens', {})
        if scene_idx not in tokens:
            self.get_scene_sample(scene_idx)
            tokens = self._sample_tokens
        token = tokens[scene_idx]

        poses = []
        cur = token
        for _ in range(11):  # 1 anchor + 10 hops -> 6 waypoints at even hops
            if not cur:
                break
            sample = self.nusc.get('sample', cur)
            lidar_sd = self.nusc.get(
                'sample_data', sample['data']['LIDAR_TOP'])
            ego_pose = self.nusc.get('ego_pose', lidar_sd['ego_pose_token'])
            poses.append(_pose_to_mat(
                ego_pose['translation'], ego_pose['rotation']))
            cur = sample['next']
        if len(poses) < 2:
            raise RuntimeError(
                f"scene {scene_idx} has <2 samples from middle; "
                "cannot build a trajectory")
        # Every 2nd pose starting at the anchor (1 s spacing).
        picks = poses[0::2][:6]
        base_inv = np.linalg.inv(picks[0])
        local = [(base_inv @ p)[:2, 3] for p in picks]
        return np.array(local, dtype=np.float32)

    def get_tracked_agents(
        self, scene_idx: int = 0, max_agents: int = 20
    ) -> Dict[str, Dict]:
        """Extract tracked-agent trajectories from annotations (replay).

        Walks the anchor sample + next 10 samples (0..5 s at 2 Hz),
        groups `sample_annotation` records by `instance_token`, and
        interpolates each track to the integer-second grid T=0..5 in
        the anchor ego frame (same grid/frame as `get_trajectory`).

        Returns:
            Dict agent_id -> {'trajectory': (6, 2) float32,
                              'parked': bool (displacement < 0.5 m),
                              'category': str}
            Empty dict on placeholder data or when nothing tracks
            long enough (>= 2 distinct times spanning >= 3 s).

        Only `vehicle.*` and `human.*` categories are kept. Positions
        are annotation box centers (global) transformed into ego
        frame -- true tracked histories, unlike CV-projected single
        detections (see docs/replay_eval_design.md).
        """
        if self.use_placeholder:
            return {}
        tokens = getattr(self, '_sample_tokens', {})
        if scene_idx not in tokens:
            self.get_scene_sample(scene_idx)
            tokens = self._sample_tokens
        token = tokens[scene_idx]

        anchor_ts = None
        base_inv = None
        tracks: Dict[str, Dict] = {}
        cur = token
        for _ in range(11):
            if not cur:
                break
            sample = self.nusc.get('sample', cur)
            lidar_sd = self.nusc.get(
                'sample_data', sample['data']['LIDAR_TOP'])
            ego_pose = self.nusc.get('ego_pose', lidar_sd['ego_pose_token'])
            ts = float(lidar_sd['timestamp']) / 1e6
            if anchor_ts is None:
                anchor_ts = ts
                base_inv = np.linalg.inv(_pose_to_mat(
                    ego_pose['translation'], ego_pose['rotation']))
            t_rel = ts - anchor_ts
            for ann_tok in sample['anns']:
                try:
                    ann = self.nusc.get('sample_annotation', ann_tok)
                except KeyError:
                    continue
                cat = str(ann.get('category_name', ''))
                if not (cat.startswith('vehicle')
                        or cat.startswith('human')):
                    continue
                inst = ann['instance_token']
                tr = tracks.setdefault(inst, {'times': [], 'pts': [],
                                              'category': cat})
                tr['times'].append(t_rel)
                tr['pts'].append((float(ann['translation'][0]),
                                  float(ann['translation'][1])))
            cur = sample['next']

        out: Dict[str, Dict] = {}
        targets = np.arange(6, dtype=float)
        for inst, tr in tracks.items():
            times = np.array(tr['times'], dtype=float)
            order = np.argsort(times)
            times = times[order]
            pts = np.array(tr['pts'], dtype=float)[order]
            if len(times) < 2 or times[-1] - times[0] < 3.0:
                continue
            xs = np.interp(targets, times, pts[:, 0])
            ys = np.interp(targets, times, pts[:, 1])
            glob = np.column_stack(
                [xs, ys, np.zeros(6), np.ones(6)])
            local = (base_inv @ glob.T).T[:, :2].astype(np.float32)
            disp = float(np.linalg.norm(local[-1] - local[0]))
            out[inst[:8]] = {'trajectory': local,
                             'parked': disp < 0.5,
                             'category': tr['category']}
            if len(out) >= max_agents:
                break
        return out
    
    def _generate_placeholder_trajectory(self, scene_idx: int) -> np.ndarray:
        """Generate placeholder trajectory.
        
        Args:
            scene_idx: Scene index (used for variation)
            
        Returns:
            Synthetic trajectory
        """
        np.random.seed(scene_idx)
        
        # Generate trajectory with some variation
        t = np.arange(6, dtype=np.float32)
        base_velocity = 5.0 + np.random.randn() * 2.0  # 5 m/s +/- 2
        
        x = t * base_velocity
        y = np.cumsum(np.random.randn(6).astype(np.float32) * 0.5)
        
        return np.column_stack([x, y])
    
    def get_map_data(self, scene_idx: int = 0) -> Dict:
        """Get map data for a scene.
        
        Args:
            scene_idx: Index of scene
            
        Returns:
            Dict with structure:
                {
                    'lanes': {
                        'lane_id': [
                            {'x1': float, 'y1': float, 'x2': float, 'y2': float}
                        ]
                    },
                    'intersections': {
                        'intersection_id': {
                            'x': float, 'y': float, 'radius': float
                        }
                    }
                }
        """
        if self.use_placeholder:
            return self._generate_placeholder_map_data(scene_idx)
        else:
            return self._load_nuscenes_map_data(scene_idx)
    
    def _load_nuscenes_map_data(self, scene_idx: int) -> Dict:
        """Build map data from the real nuScenes map expansion (N-1).

        Queries NuScenesMap around the sample ego pose (40 m radius):
          lanes: centerline segments from arcline_path_3, widths from
              polygon-edge medians (3.5 m fallback), lane_direction
              from path direction, lane_type bicycle preserved (R9.3).
          intersections: is_intersection road_segment polygons
              (centroid + max-radius, capped at 30 m).
          crosswalks/sidewalks: ped_crossing/walkway polygon bboxes.
        Deliberately OMITTED (unknown, not inventable): road_type and
        speed_limit. R1.1/R1.2/R8.3/min-speed rules needing them stay
        silent; the default 50 km/h cap still applies. The old
        hardcoded urban/13.4 ego-path lane caused GT false positives
        (e.g. scene 7); unknown-classification silence is the honest
        no-knowledge behavior. Lane dividers/boundaries, signal phases
        and stop-line roles are not extracted (logged limits of N-1).
        """
        sample = self._load_nuscenes_sample(scene_idx)
        ego_x = float(sample['ego2global'][0, 3])
        ego_y = float(sample['ego2global'][1, 3])

        scene = self.nusc.scene[scene_idx]
        log = self.nusc.get('log', scene['log_token'])
        map_name = log['location']
        self._maps = getattr(self, '_maps', {})
        if map_name not in self._maps:
            from nuscenes.map_expansion.map_api import NuScenesMap
            self._maps[map_name] = NuScenesMap(
                dataroot=str(self.data_dir), map_name=map_name)
        nmap = self._maps[map_name]

        recs = nmap.get_records_in_radius(
            ego_x, ego_y, MAP_QUERY_RADIUS,
            ['lane', 'lane_connector', 'road_segment', 'ped_crossing',
             'walkway'])

        lanes, widths, directions, lane_types = {}, {}, {}, {}
        lane_tokens = (recs.get('lane', []) +
                       recs.get('lane_connector', []))[:MAP_LANE_TOKENS]
        try:
            centerlines = nmap.discretize_lanes(lane_tokens,
                                                MAP_CENTERLINE_RES)
        except (KeyError, TypeError, ValueError):
            centerlines = {}
        for tok in lane_tokens:
            try:
                pts = [(float(x), float(y))
                       for x, y, _ in centerlines.get(tok, [])]
                if len(pts) < 2:
                    continue
                try:
                    rec = nmap.get(
                        'lane', tok, )
                except (KeyError, TypeError):
                    rec = nmap.get('lane_connector', tok)
                lid = f"lane_{tok[:8]}"
                segs = []
                for (x1, y1), (x2, y2) in zip(pts[:-1], pts[1:]):
                    if abs(x2 - x1) + abs(y2 - y1) < 1e-6:
                        continue
                    segs.append({'x1': x1, 'y1': y1, 'x2': x2, 'y2': y2})
                if not segs:
                    continue
                lanes[lid] = segs
                widths[lid] = self._lane_width_nmap(
                    nmap, rec.get('polygon_token', ''), pts)
                dx, dy = pts[-1][0] - pts[0][0], pts[-1][1] - pts[0][1]
                norm = float(np.linalg.norm([dx, dy])) or 1.0
                directions[lid] = [dx / norm, dy / norm]
                if str(rec.get('lane_type', '')).upper() == 'BIKE':
                    lane_types[lid] = 'bicycle'
            except (KeyError, TypeError, ValueError, IndexError):
                continue

        intersections = {}
        for tok in recs.get('road_segment', []):
            try:
                rs = nmap.get('road_segment', tok)
                if not rs.get('is_intersection'):
                    continue
                poly = nmap.extract_polygon(rs['polygon_token'])
                xs, ys = poly.exterior.coords.xy
                cx, cy = float(np.mean(xs)), float(np.mean(ys))
                r = min(30.0, max(
                    float(np.hypot(x - cx, y - cy))
                    for x, y in zip(xs, ys)))
                if r < 1e-6:
                    continue
                intersections[f"int_{tok[:8]}"] = {
                    'x': cx, 'y': cy, 'radius': r}
            except (KeyError, TypeError, ValueError):
                continue

        # Nearest-first ordering + caps (perf): tile count dominates
        # real-scene grounding, and far polygons cannot affect a 6-step
        # horizon anyway.
        def _nearest(layer, key, limit):
            scored = []
            for tok in recs.get(layer, []):
                try:
                    rec = nmap.get(layer, tok)
                    d2 = self._poly_dist2(
                        nmap, rec[key], ego_x, ego_y)
                except (KeyError, TypeError, ValueError):
                    continue
                scored.append((d2, tok))
            scored.sort(key=lambda t: t[0])
            return [tok for _, tok in scored[:limit]]

        crosswalks = []
        for tok in _nearest('ped_crossing', 'polygon_token',
                            MAP_CROSSWALK_POLYS):
            try:
                pc = nmap.get('ped_crossing', tok)
                for x1, y1, x2, y2 in self._tiled_boxes(
                        nmap, pc['polygon_token']):
                    crosswalks.append({'id': f"cw_{tok[:8]}",
                                       'x1': x1, 'y1': y1,
                                       'x2': x2, 'y2': y2})
            except (KeyError, TypeError, ValueError):
                continue
        sidewalks = []
        for tok in _nearest('walkway', 'polygon_token',
                            MAP_WALKWAY_POLYS):
            try:
                w = nmap.get('walkway', tok)
                for x1, y1, x2, y2 in self._tiled_boxes(
                        nmap, w['polygon_token']):
                    sidewalks.append({'id': f"sw_{tok[:8]}",
                                      'x1': x1, 'y1': y1,
                                      'x2': x2, 'y2': y2})
            except (KeyError, TypeError, ValueError):
                continue

        out = {
            'lanes': lanes,
            'lane_width': widths,
            'lane_direction': directions,
            'intersections': intersections,
        }
        if lane_types:
            out['lane_type'] = lane_types
        if crosswalks:
            out['crosswalks'] = crosswalks
        if sidewalks:
            out['sidewalks'] = sidewalks
        return self._map_to_ego_frame(out, sample['ego2global'])

    @staticmethod
    def _map_to_ego_frame(map_data: Dict, ego2global: np.ndarray) -> Dict:
        """Express map geometry in the ego-local frame.

        Trajectories (pred + GT) live in ego frame; map layers are
        global. Without this transform every lane/intersection test
        compares across frames and misfires.
        """
        inv = np.linalg.inv(np.asarray(ego2global, dtype=np.float64))
        rot = inv[:3, :3]

        def _pt(x, y):
            p = inv @ np.array([x, y, 0.0, 1.0])
            return float(p[0]), float(p[1])

        def _vec(dx, dy):
            v = rot @ np.array([dx, dy, 0.0])
            return [float(v[0]), float(v[1])]

        ego = {'lanes': {}, 'lane_width': {}, 'lane_direction': {},
               'intersections': {}}
        for lid, segs in map_data.get('lanes', {}).items():
            out_segs = []
            for seg in segs:
                x1, y1 = _pt(seg['x1'], seg['y1'])
                x2, y2 = _pt(seg['x2'], seg['y2'])
                out_segs.append({'x1': x1, 'y1': y1, 'x2': x2, 'y2': y2})
            ego['lanes'][lid] = out_segs
        ego['lane_width'] = dict(map_data.get('lane_width', {}))
        for lid, d in map_data.get('lane_direction', {}).items():
            ego['lane_direction'][lid] = _vec(d[0], d[1])
        for iid, st in map_data.get('intersections', {}).items():
            x, y = _pt(st['x'], st['y'])
            ego['intersections'][iid] = {'x': x, 'y': y,
                                         'radius': st['radius']}
        for key in ('lane_type',):
            if key in map_data:
                ego[key] = dict(map_data[key])

        def _boxes(key):
            boxes = []
            for b in map_data.get(key, []):
                x1, y1 = _pt(b['x1'], b['y1'])
                x2, y2 = _pt(b['x2'], b['y2'])
                boxes.append({'id': b['id'],
                              'x1': min(x1, x2), 'y1': min(y1, y2),
                              'x2': max(x1, x2), 'y2': max(y1, y2)})
            return boxes

        # Small rotations keep tiles near-axis-aligned; re-boxing the
        # rotated corners is a bounded approximation (documented).
        if 'crosswalks' in map_data:
            ego['crosswalks'] = _boxes('crosswalks')
        if 'sidewalks' in map_data:
            ego['sidewalks'] = _boxes('sidewalks')
        return ego

    @staticmethod
    def _lane_width_nmap(nmap, polygon_token, pts) -> float:
        """Estimate lane width: 2x median centerline-to-edge distance."""
        try:
            poly = nmap.extract_polygon(polygon_token)
            ring = list(poly.exterior.coords)
            dists = []
            for px, py in pts:
                dists.append(min(
                    float(np.hypot(px - rx, py - ry)) for rx, ry in ring))
            w = 2.0 * float(np.median(dists)) if dists else 0.0
            if 1.5 <= w <= 8.0:
                return w
        except (KeyError, TypeError, ValueError):
            pass
        return 3.5

    @staticmethod
    def _tiled_boxes(nmap, polygon_token, cell=2.5,
                     max_cells=MAP_TILE_CELLS):
        """Tile a polygon into small boxes (centers inside the polygon).

        Axis-aligned bboxes of diagonal strips cover road area and
        cause false positives; 2.5 m tiles bound the over-approximation
        to one cell. Yields (x1, y1, x2, y2) tuples, at most max_cells
        (perf: tile count dominates real-scene grounding cost).
        """
        poly = nmap.extract_polygon(polygon_token)
        from shapely.geometry import Point
        minx, miny, maxx, maxy = poly.bounds
        boxes = []
        y = miny
        while y < maxy and len(boxes) < max_cells:
            x = minx
            while x < maxx and len(boxes) < max_cells:
                if poly.contains(Point(x + cell / 2.0, y + cell / 2.0)):
                    boxes.append((x, y, min(x + cell, maxx),
                                  min(y + cell, maxy)))
                x += cell
            y += cell
        return boxes

    @staticmethod
    def _poly_dist2(nmap, polygon_token, x, y) -> float:
        """Squared distance from (x, y) to a polygon centroid (or inf)."""
        try:
            poly = nmap.extract_polygon(polygon_token)
            cx, cy = poly.centroid.x, poly.centroid.y
            return (x - cx) ** 2 + (y - cy) ** 2
        except (KeyError, TypeError, ValueError, AttributeError):
            return float('inf')
    
    def _generate_placeholder_map_data(self, scene_idx: int = 0) -> Dict:
        """Generate placeholder map data.

        Scene 9 is the HOSTILE scene (T-9/P-14): a fixed obstacle sits
        on the planner path and the red intersection is reachable, so
        placeholder runs exercise the violation -> correction loop
        instead of only the safe path. Other scenes share the base
        two-lane geometry with a reachable red at (12, 0).

        Returns:
            Synthetic map data dictionary. Lanes are axis-aligned
            centerline segments with widths/directions/types so the
            lane_discipline (R2.x) and speed_regulation (R1.x) rules
            can resolve lane membership.
        """
        out = {
            'lanes': {
                'lane_0': [
                    {'x1': -10.0, 'y1': 0.0, 'x2': 100.0, 'y2': 0.0}
                ],
                'lane_1': [
                    {'x1': -10.0, 'y1': 4.0, 'x2': 100.0, 'y2': 4.0}
                ]
            },
            'lane_width': {
                'lane_0': 4.0,
                'lane_1': 4.0,
            },
            'lane_direction': {
                'lane_0': [1.0, 0.0],
                'lane_1': [1.0, 0.0],
            },
            'road_type': {
                'lane_0': 'urban',
                'lane_1': 'urban',
            },
            'speed_limit': {
                'lane_0': 13.4,
                'lane_1': 13.4,
            },
            'lane_boundary': [
                ['lane_0', 'lane_1', 'dashed'],
            ],
            'controls_approach': {
                'int_0': 'lane_0',
            },
            'intersections': {
                # Reachable on the placeholder path (ends ~15 m):
                # T3/T4 enter the box and must hold.
                'int_0': {'x': 12.0, 'y': 0.0, 'radius': 2.0}
            }
        }
        if scene_idx == 9:
            # Stalled vehicle straddling the path centerline.
            out['fixed_obstacles'] = [
                {'id': 'obs_9', 'x1': 8.0, 'y1': -1.0,
                 'x2': 12.0, 'y2': 1.0},
            ]
        return out
    
    def get_traffic_lights(self, scene_idx: int = 0) -> Dict:
        """Get traffic light states for a scene.
        
        Args:
            scene_idx: Index of scene
            
        Returns:
            Dict with structure:
                {
                    'intersection_id': {
                        'color': 'red'|'yellow'|'green',
                        'time': int,
                        'x': float,
                        'y': float,
                        'radius': float
                    }
                }
        """
        if self.use_placeholder:
            return self._generate_placeholder_traffic_lights(scene_idx)
        else:
            # nuScenes provides no traffic-signal states. Return empty
            # (no knowledge) rather than inventing phases: signal rules
            # stay silent, which is the honest behavior.
            return {}
    
    def _generate_placeholder_traffic_lights(
        self, scene_idx: int = 0
    ) -> Dict:
        """Generate placeholder traffic light states.

        The red intersection matches the placeholder map (reachable at
        (12, 0), T3/T4 entry). Scene 9 keeps the same red on top of
        its obstacle.

        Returns:
            Synthetic traffic light dictionary
        """
        return {
            'int_0': {
                'color': 'red',
                # Span the horizon so entry (not just T0) is checked;
                # T0 starts outside the box (see map placeholder).
                'times': [0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5],
                'x': 12.0,
                'y': 0.0,
                'radius': 2.0
            }
        }

==> asp-safety/src/evaluation.py <==
"""Evaluation Metrics for ASP Safety Layer.

This module provides the MetricsCalculator class for evaluating
trajectory planning performance.
"""

import numpy as np
from typing import List, Dict, Tuple


class MetricsCalculator:
    """Calculate evaluation metrics for trajectory planning.
    
    This class provides static methods for calculating various
    metrics used to evaluate autonomous driving performance.
    """
    
    @staticmethod
    def collision_rate(
        trajectories: List[np.ndarray],
        gt_boxes: List[np.ndarray],
        pred_boxes: List[np.ndarray]
    ) -> float:
        """Calculate collision rate.
        
        Args:
            trajectories: List of predicted trajectories
                Each shape: (T, 2)
            gt_boxes: List of ground truth bounding boxes
                Each shape: (N, 7) - [x, y, z, w, l, h, yaw]
                (canonical layout, same as SparseDrivePlanner)
            pred_boxes: List of predicted bounding boxes
                Each shape: (M, 7) - [x, y, z, w, l, h, yaw]
                
        Returns:
            float: Collision rate as percentage [0.0, 100.0]

        Formula:
            collision_rate = (num_collisions / total_trajectories) * 100

        Boxes are static single-frame detections (no timestamps);
        trajectory index is the time axis. Moving-agent awareness
        lives in the checker's predicted_pos path, not here.

        Raises:
            ValueError: If the three lists have different lengths (the
                old zip-truncation silently dropped scenes).
        """
        if not trajectories:
            return 0.0

        if not (len(trajectories) == len(gt_boxes) == len(pred_boxes)):
            raise ValueError(
                f"mismatched batch lengths: {len(trajectories)}, "
                f"{len(gt_boxes)}, {len(pred_boxes)}"
            )

        collisions = 0
        for traj, gt, pred in zip(trajectories, gt_boxes, pred_boxes):
            if MetricsCalculator._check_collision(traj, gt, pred):
                collisions += 1

        return collisions / len(trajectories) * 100
    
    @staticmethod
    def l2_error(
        pred_trajectory: np.ndarray,
        gt_trajectory: np.ndarray
    ) -> float:
        """Calculate L2 displacement error.
        
        Args:
            pred_trajectory: Predicted trajectory, shape (T, 2)
            gt_trajectory: Ground truth trajectory, shape (T', 2)
            
        Returns:
            float: Average L2 error in meters
            
        Formula:
            l2_error = mean(sqrt((pred_x - gt_x)^2 + (pred_y - gt_y)^2))

        Raises:
            ValueError: On non-(T, 2) shapes or non-finite values
                (NaN/inf previously propagated silently into means).
        """
        pred_trajectory = np.asarray(pred_trajectory, dtype=float)
        gt_trajectory = np.asarray(gt_trajectory, dtype=float)
        for name, arr in (("pred_trajectory", pred_trajectory),
                          ("gt_trajectory", gt_trajectory)):
            if arr.ndim != 2 or arr.shape[1] != 2:
                raise ValueError(
                    f"{name} must have shape (T, 2), got {arr.shape}")
            if not np.all(np.isfinite(arr)):
                raise ValueError(f"{name} contains NaN/inf")

        min_len = min(len(pred_trajectory), len(gt_trajectory))

        if min_len == 0:
            return 0.0

        return float(np.mean(np.linalg.norm(
            pred_trajectory[:min_len] - gt_trajectory[:min_len], axis=1
        )))
    
    @staticmethod
    def _matches(true_v: Dict, detected_v: Dict) -> bool:
        """Match two violation dicts (P-17).

        Rule + timestep must agree; vehicle/vehicle2/type must agree
        whenever BOTH dicts carry the key (hand-made legacy dicts
        without them still match by rule+time).
        """
        if (true_v.get('rule') != detected_v.get('rule')
                or true_v.get('time') != detected_v.get('time')):
            return False
        for key in ('vehicle', 'vehicle2', 'type'):
            if key in true_v and key in detected_v:
                if true_v[key] != detected_v[key]:
                    return False
        return True

    @staticmethod
    def violation_detection_rate(
        true_violations: List[Dict],
        detected_violations: List[Dict]
    ) -> float:
        """Calculate true positive rate for violation detection.
        
        Args:
            true_violations: List of actual violations
            detected_violations: List of detected violations
            
        Returns:
            float: Detection rate [0.0, 1.0]
            
        Formula:
            detection_rate = true_positives / total_true_violations
        """
        if not true_violations:
            return 1.0 if not detected_violations else 0.0
        
        detected = 0
        for tv in true_violations:
            for dv in detected_violations:
                if MetricsCalculator._matches(tv, dv):
                    detected += 1
                    break

        return detected / len(true_violations)

    @staticmethod
    def false_discovery_rate(
        true_violations: List[Dict],
        detected_violations: List[Dict]
    ) -> float:
        """Calculate false discovery rate for violation detection.

        FDR = false_positives / total_detected_violations (the
        statistically correct name; false_positive_rate is kept as an
        alias for backward compatibility).

        Args:
            true_violations: List of actual violations
            detected_violations: List of detected violations

        Returns:
            float: False discovery rate [0.0, 1.0]
        """
        if not detected_violations:
            return 0.0

        false_positives = 0
        for dv in detected_violations:
            is_true = False
            for tv in true_violations:
                if MetricsCalculator._matches(tv, dv):
                    is_true = True
                    break
            if not is_true:
                false_positives += 1

        return false_positives / len(detected_violations)

    @staticmethod
    def false_positive_rate(
        true_violations: List[Dict],
        detected_violations: List[Dict]
    ) -> float:
        """Alias of false_discovery_rate (backward compatibility)."""
        return MetricsCalculator.false_discovery_rate(
            true_violations, detected_violations)
    
    @staticmethod
    def average_inference_time(inference_times: List[float]) -> float:
        """Calculate average inference time.
        
        Args:
            inference_times: List of inference times in milliseconds
            
        Returns:
            float: Average inference time in ms
        """
        if not inference_times:
            return 0.0
        return float(np.mean(inference_times))
    
    @staticmethod
    def _check_collision(
        traj: np.ndarray,
        gt_boxes: np.ndarray,
        pred_boxes: np.ndarray
    ) -> bool:
        """Check if trajectory collides with any box.
        
        Args:
            traj: Trajectory (T, 2)
            gt_boxes: Ground truth boxes (N, 7)
            pred_boxes: Predicted boxes (M, 7)
            
        Returns:
            bool: True if collision detected
        """
        for t, point in enumerate(traj):
            for box in gt_boxes:
                if MetricsCalculator._point_in_box(point, box):
                    return True
            for box in pred_boxes:
                if MetricsCalculator._point_in_box(point, box):
                    return True
        return False
    
    @staticmethod
    def _point_in_box(point: np.ndarray, box: np.ndarray) -> bool:
        """Check if 2D point is inside a yawed bounding box (P-17).

        Args:
            point: (x, y) point
            box: Bounding box [x, y, z, w, l, h, yaw]
                Footprint uses l (x-extent) x w (y-extent); h is
                vertical and ignored. Yaw rotates the footprint about
                z (right-hand rule); the point is tested in the box
                frame. Boxes shorter than 7 elements never match.

        Returns:
            bool: True if point is inside box
        """
        if len(box) < 7:
            return False

        import math
        x, y = float(point[0]), float(point[1])
        bx, by = float(box[0]), float(box[1])
        bw, bl = float(box[3]), float(box[4])
        yaw = float(box[6])

        dx, dy = x - bx, y - by
        cos_y, sin_y = math.cos(yaw), math.sin(yaw)
        lx, ly = dx * cos_y + dy * sin_y, -dx * sin_y + dy * cos_y

        return abs(lx) <= bl / 2 and abs(ly) <= bw / 2

==> asp-safety/src/neural_planner.py <==
"""SparseDrive Neural Planner Wrapper.

This module provides a wrapper class for SparseDrive inference.
The class loads pre-trained weights and generates trajectory predictions.

SparseDrive outputs:
- final_planning: (6, 2) - cumulative (x,y) at t=0.5,1.0,1.5,2.0,2.5,3.0s
- boxes_3d: (N, 7) - [x, y, z, w, l, h, yaw] in lidar frame
- scores_3d: (N,) - detection confidence scores

Integration requires:
1. SparseDrive repo cloned (done)
2. Custom CUDA ops compiled: cd SparseDrive/projects/mmdet3d_plugin/ops && python setup.py develop
3. mmdet3d dependencies installed (see SparseDrive/requirement.txt)
4. Pre-trained weights downloaded
"""

import numpy as np
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Dict, Optional, List


class SparseDrivePlanner:
    """Wrapper for SparseDrive model inference.
    
    This class loads a pre-trained SparseDrive model and provides
    a simple interface for trajectory prediction.
    
    The model outputs trajectory as cumulative (x, y) positions in the
    ego/lidar coordinate frame at 0.5s intervals for 3 seconds ahead.
    
    Attributes:
        model_path: Path to .pth weights file
        device: PyTorch device ("cuda" or "cpu")
        model: Loaded SparseDrive model (None if not loaded)
        use_placeholder: Whether using placeholder data
    """
    
    # SparseDrive output constants
